                           2011 DATABASE MCQ MARKING

   1. Which of the following database types is widely used in computer applications?
      mß.Kl fhÿï i|yd nyq,j Ndú; jk o;a; iuqod (Database) j¾.h jkqfha

          1) Flat file                  2) Hierarchical               3) Network                    4) Relational                 5) Object oriented
             me;,sf.dkq                    OQrdj,s                       cd,                           iïnkaOl                       jia;= keUqre

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2011 – 07 )
          Answer : 4 )

          Explanation
          meyeÈ,s lsÍu

          Option / úl,amh 1)
          Flat file databases store data in plain text formats like .csv or .txt, with simple structures such as rows and
          columns. They are easy to create and use for small-scale tasks, but they lack relationships between data,
          indexing, and multi-user support. As a result, they are not suitable for complex or large-scale applications.
          me;,s f.dkq o;a; iuqodhka .csv fyda .txt jeks ir, fm< wdlD;sj, o;a; .nvd lrhs" fma<s iy ;Sre
          jeks ir, jHqyhka iuÕ' tAjd ks¾udKh lsÍug iy l=vd mßudK ld¾hhka i|yd Ndú;d lsÍug
          myiqh" kuq;a tAjdg o;a;" iqÑ.; lsÍu iy nyq-mßYS,l iydh w;r iïnkaO;d fkdue;' tys m%;sM,hla
          f,i" tAjd ixlS¾K fyda úYd, mßudK fhÿï i|yd iqÿiq fkdfõ'

          Option / úl,amh 2)
          Hierarchical databases organize data in a tree-like structure with parent-child relationships. While they
          were widely used in early systems like IBM’s IMS, their rigid structure makes it difficult to handle
          complex relationships or perform flexible queries. They are rarely used in modern applications.
          OQrdj,s o;a; iuqodhka udmsh-<ud in|;d iys; .ila jeks jHqyhl o;a; ixúOdkh lrhs' IBM ys
          IMS jeks uq,a moaO;sj, tAjd nyq,j Ndú;d jqjo" tAjdfha oDv jHqyh ixlS¾K iïnkaO;d yeisrùug
          fyda kuHYS,S úuiqï isÿ lsÍug wmyiq lrhs' tAjd kùk fhÿïj, l,d;=rlska Ndú;d fõ'

          Option / úl,amh 3)
          Network databases allow more complex relationships using a graph-like structure with many-to-many
          connections. Though more flexible than hierarchical models, they are difficult to manage and query. Their
          use has declined significantly with the rise of relational and NoSQL databases.
          cd, o;a; iuqodhka nyq-nyq iïnkaO;d iys; m%ia:dr jeks jHqyhla Ndú;d lrñka jvd;a ixlS¾K
          iïnkaO;d j,g bv i,ihs' OQrdj,s wdlD;sj,g jvd kuHYS,S jqjo" tAjd l<ukdlrKh lsÍug iy
          úuiqï lsÍug wmyiqh' iïnkaO;d iy NoSQL o;a; iuqodhka by< hdu;a iuÕ tAjdfha Ndú;h
          ie,lsh hq;= f,i wvq ù we;'

          Option / úl,amh 4)
          Relational databases store data in tables with rows and columns, allowing relationships through keys. They
          support powerful querying with SQL and ensure data integrity and consistency. Due to their efficiency,
          scalability, and flexibility, they are the most widely used database type in modern computer applications.
          So, this is the correct answer.
          iïnkaO;d o;a; iuqodhka fma<s iy ;Sre iys; j.= j, o;a; .nvd lrhs" h;=re yryd iïnkaO;d
          j,g bv fohs' tAjd SQL iuÕ n,j;a úuiqï i|yd iyh olajk w;r o;a; wLKav;dj iy
          wkql+,;dj iy;sl lrhs' tAjdfha ld¾hlaIu;dj" mßudKh lsÍfï yelshdj iy kuHYS,S nj ksid" tAjd
          kùk mß.Kl fhÿïj, nyq,j Ndú;d jk o;a; iuqod j¾.h fõ' tneúka" fuh ksjerÈ ms<s;=rhs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                              -2-                                        #ictfromabc



    Option / úl,amh 5)
    Object-oriented databases store data in the form of objects, closely integrating with object-oriented
    programming languages. They are useful for applications dealing with complex data like multimedia or
    engineering systems. However, they are not as common or widely supported as relational databases.
    jia;=-keUqre o;a; iuqodhka jia;=-keUqre l%uf,aLk NdId iuÕ iómj tAldnoaO fjñka jia;= wdldrfhka
    o;a; .nvd lrhs' nyqudOH fyda bxðfkare moaO;s jeks ixlS¾K o;a; iuÕ lghq;= lrk fhÿï i|yd
    tAjd m%fhdackj;a fõ' flfia fj;;a" tAjd iïnkaO;d o;a; iuqodhka ;rï fmdÿ fyda mq¿,a f,i iydh
    fkdolajhs'

    Therefore, option 4) is the correct answer.

 2. Consider the following statements about computerized databases:
    mß.Kl o;a; iuqod (Computerized databases) ms<sn|j my; oelafjk m%ldY i,ld n,kak'
    A. Need more human resources to manage the computerized database than a manual system.
       mß.Kl .; o;a; iuqodh l<ukdlrKh i|yd yia;Sh moaO;shlg (Manual system) jvd
       jeäfhka udkj iïm;a wjYH fõ'
    B. Retrieval of data is efficient than a manual system.
       o;a; wdmiq ,nd.ekSu (Retrieval), yia;Sh moaO;shlg jvd ld¾hlaIu fõ'
    C. No data duplications
       o;a; oaú;ajlrK (Duplications) fkdue;'
    D. Need more space to store data than a manual system.
       o;a; .nvdlsÍu i|yd yia;Sh moaO;shlg jvd bvlv wjYH fõ'
    Which of the above statements are correct with respect to a properly designed database?
    ukd f,i ie,iqïlrk ,o o;a; iuqodhla iïnkaOfhka i;H jkafka by; i|yka ljr m%ldY o?

    1) A and B only.    2) A and D only. 3) B and C only.    4) B and D only.     5) C and D only.
       A yd B muKs.        A yd D muKs.     B yd C muKs.        B yd D muKs.         C yd D muKs.

                                                                                              ( 2011 – 23 )
    Answer : 3 )

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    This is false. A computerized database typically automates many tasks such as data entry, updates,
    searches, and backups. Therefore, it reduces the need for large numbers of staff compared to a manual
    system where each task must be performed by hand. Once set up, fewer people can manage more
    data efficiently.
    fuh wi;Hhls' mß.Kl.; o;a; iuqodhla idudkHfhka o;a; we;=<;a lsÍu" hdj;ald,Sk lsÍï" fiùï
    iy Wmia: jeks fndfyda ld¾hhka iajhxl%Sh lrhs' tneúka" tla tla ld¾hh w;ska isÿ l< hq;= w;ska
    moaO;shlg idfmalaIj úYd, ld¾h uKav,hla i|yd wjYH;djh th wvq lrhs' ieliQ miq" wvq
    msßilg jeä o;a; ld¾hlaIuj l<ukdlrKh l< yelsh'

    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    This is true. In a computerized database, data can be retrieved instantly using queries and filters. In
    contrast, a manual system would require physically searching through files or documents, which is
    time-consuming. Thus, computerized databases significantly improve data access speed.
    fuh i;Hhls' mß.Kl.; o;a; iuqodhl" úuiqï iy fmryka Ndú;fhka laIKslj o;a; ,nd .;
    yelsh' Bg fjkiaj" w;ska moaO;shlg ,smsf.dkq fyda f,aLk yryd fN!;slj fiùu wjYH jkq we;"
    th ld,h .;jk fohls' fï wkqj" mß.Kl.; o;a; iuqodhka o;a; m%fõY fõ.h ie,lsh hq;= f,i
    jeä ÈhqKq lrhs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -3-                                      #ictfromabc


     Statement / m%ldYh C)
     This is true when the database is properly designed. A well-structured database uses normalization
     to organize data efficiently, which reduces or eliminates unnecessary duplication. Unique constraints
     and primary keys also help ensure data is not repeated accidentally.
     o;a; iuqodh ksis f,i ks¾udKh lr we;s úg fuh i;H fõ' fyd¢ka jHqy.; o;a; iuqodhla o;a;
     ld¾hlaIuj ixúOdkh lsÍu i|yd idudkHlrKh Ndú;d lrhs" th wkjYH wkqmsgm;a wvq lrhs fyda
     bj;a lrhs' woaú;Sh iSudjka iy m%d:ñl h;=re o o;a; wyïfnka kej; isÿ fkdjk nj iy;sl
     lsÍug WmldÍ fõ'

     Statement / m%ldYh D)
     This is false. Computerized databases require digital storage, which can hold massive amounts of
     data in a very small physical space. Manual systems need large physical areas like cabinets, drawers,
     and rooms to store paper records, making them far less space efficient.
     fuh wi;Hhls' mß.Kl.; o;a; iuqodhkag äðg,a .nvdjla wjYH jk w;r tuÕska b;d l=vd
     fN!;sl wjldYhl oejeka; o;a; m%udKhla r|jd .; yelsh' w;ska moaO;sj,g lvodis jd¾;d .nvd
     lsÍu i|yd leìkÜ" ,dÉpq iy ldur jeks úYd, fN!;sl m%foaY wjYH jk w;r tuÕska tAjd
     wjldYh-ld¾hlaIu;djh fnfyúka wvq lrhs'

     Therefore, the correct answer is 3) B and C only.
     tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 3) B iy C muKs'

 •   Consider the following system description and the relations A, B, C and D given below to answer
     the questions 03 and 04
     wxl 03 yd 04 m%Yakj,g ms<s;=re iemhSu i|yd my; i|yka moaO;s úia;rh yd A, B, C, D iïnkaO
     (Relation) i,lkak'

     A principal of a National school wants to develop a database to maintain Admission Number,
     Student Name, Address, National Identity Card number (NIC) and the Date of Birth (DOB) of
     Advanced Level students. The principal also wants to know the marks obtained for each subject by
     the students. In addition to the above requirements, the principal needs to know the subjects assigned
     to the teachers.
     cd;sl mdi,l úÿy,am;sjrfhl=g, Wiia fm< isiqkaf.a we;=<;aùfï wxlh, YsIH kduh, ,smskh, cd;sl
     yeÿkqïm;a wxlh (NIC) iy Wmka Èkh mj;ajd .ekSu i|yd o;a; iuqodhla ilia lr .ekSug wjYH
     fõ' tla tla úIhh i|yd YsIHhd ,nd .;a ,l=Kq o oek.ekSug úÿy,am;sjrhdg wjYH fjhs' by;
     wjYH;dj,g wu;rj .=rejrekag mjrd we;s úIhhka oek.ekSugo úÿy,am;sjrhdg wjYH fjhs'

     Relations / iïnkaO:
     A. Student(admissionNo, studentName, address, DOB, NIC)
     B. Subject(subjectCode, subjectName)
     C. Mark(admissionNo, subjectCode, marksObtained)
     D. Teacher(teacherNo, subjectCode, teacherName, subjectName, class)

 3. Which of the above relations are in the third normal form?
    by; oelafjk iïnkaO w;=frka 3 jk m%u; wdlD;sfhys (3rd Normal Form) we;af;a ljrla o?

     1) A and C only.   2) A and D only. 3) A, B and C only. 4) A, C and D only. 5) B, C and D only.
        A yd C muKs.       A yd D muKs.     A, B yd C muKs.     A, C yd D muKs.     B, C yd D muKs.

                                                                                              ( 2011 – 24 )
     Answer: 3)

     Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
     Let’s examine each relation and apply the rules of 3rd Normal Form.
     tla tla iïnkaO;djh mÍlaId lr 3 jk m%u; wdlD;sfha kS;s wod< lr .ksuq'

     A relation is in 3NF if / iïnkaO;djhla 3NF ys ;sfí kï
     It should be in 2NF / th 2NF ys ;sìh hq;=h'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -4-                                        #ictfromabc


    It shall not contain any transitive dependencies. It means non-key attributes depend only on the primary
    key, not on other non-key attributes.
    tys lsisÿ ixl%dka;s mrdh;a;;djhla wvx.= fkdúh hq;=h' tys f;areu h;=re fkdjk .=Kdx. m%d:ñl
    h;=r u; muKla r|d mj;sk w;r wfkl=;a h;=re fkdjk .=Kdx. u; fkdfõ'
    Explanation of each relation / tla tla iïnkaO;djh meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    Relation / iïnkaO;djh A – Student
    This relation uses admissionNo as the primary key, and all other attributes like studentName, address,
    DOB, and NIC depend directly on it. There are no transitive dependencies. Therefore, it is in 3NF.
    fuu iïnkaO;djh m%d:ñl h;=r f,i admissionNo Ndú;d lrk w;r studentName" address" DOB
    iy NIC jeks wfkl=;a ish¨u .=Kdx. th u; fl,skau r|d mj;S' ixl%dka;s mrdh;a;;d fkdue;'
    tneúka" th 3NF ys we;'
    Relation / iïnkaO;djh B – Subject
    Here, subjectCode is the primary key, and subjectName depends entirely on it. There are no unnecessary
    dependencies or redundant data. Therefore, it is in 3NF.
    fuys" subjectCode hkq m%d:ñl h;=r jk w;r subjectName iïmQ¾Kfhkau tA u; r|d mj;S' wkjYH
    hemSï fyda w;sßla; o;a; fkdue;' tneúka" th 3NF ys we;'

    Relation / iïnkaO;djh C – Mark
    This relation has a composite key (admissionNo, subjectCode), and marksObtained depends on the full
    combination of both keys. No partial or transitive dependencies exist. Therefore, it is in 3NF.
    fuu iïnkaO;djh ixhqla; h;=rla we; ^admissionNo" subjectCode&" iy marksObtained h;=re
    foflysu iïmQ¾K ixfhdackh u; r|d mj;S' w¾O fyda ixl%dka;s mrdh;a;;d fkdue;' tneúka" th
    3NF ys we;'

    Relation / iïnkaO;djh D – Teacher
    This relation includes dependencies like subjectName depending on subjectCode, and teacherName
    depending on teacherNo. Since not all non-key attributes depend only on the primary key, transitive
    dependencies are present. Therefore, it is not in 3NF.
    fuu iïnkaO;djhg úIh fla;h u; mokïj subjectName iy teacherName u; mokïj teacherNo
    jeks mrdh;a;;d we;=<;a fõ' ish¨u h;=re fkdjk .=Kdx. m%d:ñl h;=r u; muKla r|d fkdmj;sk
    neúka" ixl%dka;s mrdh;a;;d mj;S' tneúka" th 3NF ys fkdue;'

    So, the correct answer is 3) A, B and C only.
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 3) A, B iy C muKs'

 4. Which of the following combinations of attributes provides the minimal set of primary keys for the
    relations Student, Subject and Mark respectively?
    my; i|yka Wm,laIK ixfhdack w;=frka ms<sfj<ska YsIHhd (Student), úIhh (Subject), yd ,l=Kq
    (Mark) iïnkaO ms<sn|j wju m%d:ñl h;=re (Primary key) l=,lh fokq ,nkafka l=ulska o?

    1)   admissionNo and NIC, subjectCode, admissionNo.
    2)   NIC, subjectCode, subjectCode.
    3)   admissionNo, subjectCode, subjectCode.
    4)   admissionNo, subjectCode, admissionNo and subjectCode.
    5)   admissionNo, subjectName, admissionNo and subjectCode.

                                                                                                ( 2011 – 25 )
    Answer : 4 )

    Let’s valuate the minimal set of primary keys for the relations Student, Subject, and Mark
    Student" Subject iy Mark iïnkaO;d i|yd wju m%d:ñl h;=re lÜg,h ;lafiare lruq

    Student(admissionNo, studentName, address, DOB, NIC)
    admissionNo is unique and used as the identifier. NIC is also unique in real life but not part of this
    relation’s design as a key. So, here the minimal primary key is admissionNo.
    admissionNo wkkH jk w;r th y÷kd.ekSu f,i Ndú;d lrhs' NIC ienE Ôú;fha§ o wkkH
    fõ" kuq;a h;=rla f,i fuu iïnkaO;djfha ie,iqfï fldgila fkdfõ' tneúka" fuys wju m%d:ñl
    h;=r admissionNo fõ'

    Subject(subjectCode, subjectName)
    subjectCode uniquely identifies a subject. So, here the minimal primary key is subjectCode.
    subjectCode úIhhla wkkH f,i y÷kd .kS' tneúka" fuys wju m%d:ñl h;=r subjectCode fõ'

    Mark(admissionNo, subjectCode, marksObtained)
    A student can receive a mark for each subject, So, here the minimal primary key is a composite key,
    which is admissionNo and subjectCode.
    YsIHfhl=g iEu úIhhla i|ydu ,l=Kla ,nd .; yelsh" tneúka" fuys wju m%d:ñl h;=r ixhqla;
    h;=rls" th admissionNo iy subjectCode fõ'

    Therefore, the correct answer is 4) admissionNo, subjectCode, admissionNo, subjectCode.
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 4) admissionNo, subjectCode, admissionNo, subjectCode fõ'

    This matches exactly with the minimal primary keys for Student, Subject, and Mark.
    fuh Student" Subject iy Mark i|yd wju m%d:ñl h;=re iuÕ yßhgu .e,fma'

 5. Consider the following statements with respect to ER diagrams:
    ER igyka ms<sn|j my; oelafjk m%ldY i,lkak'
    A. An ER diagram has entities and relationships.
       ER igykl N+;d¾: (Entities) iy iïnkaO;d (Relationships) we;'
    B. Cardinality of all relationships should always be one-to-one.
       ish¨ iïnkaO;dj, .KkSh;dj (Cardinality) ieuúgu tlg tl (One-to-One) úh hq;=h'
    C. Entities may have attributes
       N+;d¾:j,g wkq,laIK (Attributes) ;sìh yelsh'
    D. There could be binary and tertiary relationships.
       tajdfha oaúuh (Binary) iy ;D;Sh (Teritary) iïnkaO;d ;sìh yelsh'
    Which of the above statements are correct?
    by; m%ldY w;=frka ksjeros jkafka ljr tajd o?

    1) A and D only. 2) B and C only.       3) B and D only. 4) A, C and D only. 5) B, C and D only.
       A yd D muKs.     B yd C muKs.           B yd D muKs.     A, C yd D muKs.     B, C yd D muKs.

                                                                                              ( 2011 – 43 )
    Answer : 4 )

    Let's evaluate each statement about ER (Entity-Relationship) diagrams
    ER ^N=;d¾:-iïnkaO;d& rEm igyka ms<sn| tla tla m%ldYh we.hSug ,la lruq

    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    Correct. ER diagrams represent data models using entities, attributes, and relationships.
    ksjerÈhs' ER rEm igyka uÕska N=;d¾:" .=Kdx. iy iïnkaO;d Ndú;d lrñka o;a; wdlD;s
    ksrEmKh flf¾'

    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    Incorrect. Cardinality can be one-to-one, one-to-many and many-to-many. Therefore, saying it should
    always be one-to-one is incorrect.
    jerÈhs' .Kksh;djh tAl-tAl" tal-nyq iy nyq-nyq úh yelsh' tneúka" th ieuúgu tAl-tAl úh
    hq;= nj mejiSu jerÈhs'

    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    Correct. Entities typically do have attributes, like name, ID, etc.
    ksjerÈhs' N=;d¾:j,g idudkHfhka ku" ID jeks .=Kdx. we;'

    Statement / m%ldYh D)
    Correct. Relationships in ER diagrams can be binary (between 2 entities), ternary (among 3 entities) or
    n-ary (among n number of entities).
    ksjerÈhs' ER rEm igyka j, iïnkaO;d oaúuh ^N=;d¾: 2 la w;r&" ;%s;aj ^N=;d¾: 3 la w;r& fyda
    n-ary ^N=;d¾: n .Kkla w;r& úh yelsh'

    The correct statements are A, C, and D.
    ksjerÈ m%ldY A, C, iy D fõ'

    Therefore, the correct answer is 4) A, C and D only.
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 4) A, C iy D muKs'

                          2012 DATABASE MCQ MARKING

   1. Which of the following statements is correct about relational databases?
      iïnkaOl o;a; iuqod (relational databases) ms<sn| j my; olafjk ljr m%ldYh ksjerÈ fõo?

          1) The primary key of a table is selected from alternate keys.
             j.=jl m%d:ñl h;=r (primary key) úl,am h;=re (alternate keys) w;=frka f;dard .efka'
          2) Duplicate values are not allowed in a foreign key field of a table.
             j.=jl wkH h;=rla (a foreign key) i|yd w.hka wkqmsgm;a lsÍu (duplicate) l< fkdyels h'
          3) A table cannot be created without a primary key.
             m%d:ñl h;=rla fkdue;sj j.=jla ks¾udKh l< fkdyels h
          4) A foreign key of a table should be the primary key of another. table,
             j.=jl wkH h;=rla fjk;a j.=jl m%d:ñl h;=rla úh hq;= h'
          5) A table should have alternate keys
             j.=jl úl,am h;=re ;sìh hq;=u h'

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2012 – 07 )
          Answer : 4 )

          Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

          Option/ úl,amh 1)
          Incorrect. An alternate key is actually any candidate key that is not selected as the primary key. So, first,
          we identify candidate keys (columns or sets of columns that can uniquely identify rows), then choose one
          as the primary key, and the rest become alternate keys.
          jerÈhs' úl,am h;=rla hkq m%d:ñl h;=r f,i f;dard fkd.;a ´kEu wfmalaIl h;=rls' tneúka" m<uqj"
          wfmalaIl h;=re y÷kd .ksuq ^fma<s wkkHj y÷kd.; yels ;Sre fyda ;Sre lÜg,&" miqj tlla m%d:ñl
          h;=r f,i f;dard .ksuq" b;sßh úl,am h;=re njg m;afõ'

          Option/ úl,amh 2)
          Incorrect. A foreign key can have duplicate values. It just has to refer to valid values in the referenced
          table’s primary key. For example, many students can have the same class ID referencing one class.
          jerÈhs' wd.ka;=l h;=rlg wkqmsgm;a w.hka ;sìh yelsh' th fhduq lrk ,o j.=fõ m%d:ñl h;=f¾
          j,x.= w.hka fj; fhduq l< hq;=h' WodyrKhla f,i" fndfyda isiqkag tla mka;shla fhduq lrñka
          tlu mka;s ID tlla ;sìh yelsh'

          Option/ úl,amh 3)
          Incorrect. Although it is good design to have a primary key, relational databases (like MySQL,
          PostgreSQL, etc.) allow you to create a table without a primary key. It is not mandatory in SQL standards,
          though it is recommended.
          jerÈhs' m%d:ñl h;=rla ;sîu fyd| ie,iqula jqjo" iïnkaO;d o;a; iuqodhka ^MySQL, PostgreSQL,
          wdÈh& m%d:ñl h;=rla fkdue;sj j.=jla ks¾udKh lsÍug Tng bv i,ihs' th ks¾foaY lr we;;a"
          SQL m%ñ;Skays th wksjd¾h fkdfõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -8-                                           #ictfromabc



    Option/ úl,amh 4)
    Correct. A foreign key refers to the primary key (or a unique key) of another table to ensure referential
    integrity. So, it is true that a foreign key should reference the primary key of another table (or a unique
    constraint field).
    ksjerÈhs' fhduq wLKav;dj iy;sl lsÍu i|yd wd.ka;=l h;=rla fjk;a j.=jl m%d:ñl h;=r ^fyda
    wkkH h;=rla& fj; fhduq fõ' tneúka" wd.ka;=l h;=rla fjk;a j.=jl ^fyda wkkH iSud lafIa;%hl&
    m%d:ñl h;=r fhduq l< hq;= nj i;Hhls'

    Option/ úl,amh 5)
    Incorrect. A table can have alternate keys, but it is not mandatory. There can be only one candidate key,
    which becomes the primary key, so there may be no alternate keys at all.
    jerÈhs' j.=jlg úl,am h;=re ;sìh yels kuq;a th wksjd¾h fkdfõ' tla wfmalaIl h;=rla muKla
    ;sìh yels w;r th m%d:ñl h;=r njg m;afõ" tneúka úl,am h;=re lsisfia;a fkd;sìh yelsh'

    Therefore, option 4) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 4) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 2. Consider the following statements about Relational Databases:

 3. Consider the following statements with respect to ER diagrams:
    ER rEmigyka ms<sn|j my; olafjk m%ldY i,ld n,kak(
    A. A relationship in an ER diagram can be implemented as a table in a relational database.
       ER rEmigykl iïnkaO;djla (relationship), iïnkaOl iuqodhl (relational database) j.=jla
       (table) f,i ia:dms; l< yelsh'
    B. A relationship may have attributes attached to it.
       iïnkaO;djlg, thg iïnkaO jQ Wm,laIK (attributes) ;sìh yelsh'
    C. Only two entities can be attached to a relationship.
       iïnkaO;djlg, N+;d¾: (entities) folla muKla iïnkaO l< yels h'

    Which of the above statements is/are correct?
    by; m%ldY w;=frka l=uk m%ldYh$m%ldY i;H fõ o?

    1) A only.         2) B only.          3) A and B only.   4) A and C only.     5) B and C only.
       A muKs.            B muKs.             A yd B muKs.       A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.

                                                                                               ( 2012 – 23 )
    Answer: 3)

    Evaluating each statement / tla tla m%ldYh meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    True. In ER modelling, relationships describe associations between entities. When converting an ER
    diagram into a relational database schema, relationships are often implemented as tables. One-to-one and
    one-to-many relationships may result in exchange of foreign keys with already existing tables instead of
    creating new tables, but many-to-many relationships always result in a separate table.
    i;Hhs' ER wdlD;s ks¾udKfha§" iïnkaO;d u.ska N=;d¾: w;r iïnkaO;d úia;r flf¾' ER rEm
    igykla iïnkaO;d o;a; iuqod fhdackd l%uhla njg mßj¾;kh lsÍfï§" iïnkaO;d fndfyda úg j.=
    f,i l%shd;aul fõ' tAl-tAl iy tAl-nyq iïnkaO;d kj j.= ks¾udKh lsÍu fjkqjg oekgu;a mj;sk
    j.= iuÕ wd.ka;=l h;=re yqjudre lr .ekSug fya;= úh yel" kuq;a nyq-nyq iïnkaO;d iEu úgu
    fjku j.=jlg fya;= fõ'

    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    True. In ER diagrams, relationships can have attributes. These are called descriptive attributes.
    i;Hhs' ER rEm igyka j," iïnkaO;dj,g .=Kdx. ;sìh yelsh' fïjd úia;rd;aul Wm,laIK f,i
    ye¢kafõ'

    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    False. A relationship can involve more than two entities (ternary or n-ary relationships).
    wi;Hhs' iïnkaO;djhlg N=;d¾: follg jvd ^;%s;aj fyda n-ary iïnkaO;d& we;=<;a úh yelsh'

    Therefore, the correct answer is 3) A and B only.
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 3) A iy B muKs fõ'

 4. Which of the following statements about relational databases is correct?
    iïnkaOl o;a;iuqod ms<sn|j my; oelafjk ljr m%ldYh i;H fõ o?

    1) A value of a composite key of a table can be null.
       j.=jl ixhqla; h;=rl (composite key) w.h wNsY=kH (null) úh yels h'
    2) Null values are allowed in any column of a table.
       j.=jl ´kEu ;Srejl wNsY=kH (null) w.hhka ;sìh yels h'
    3) Two or more columns of a table can be combined to create the primary key.
       m%d:ñl h;=rla ks¾udKh lsÍu i|yd j.=jl ;Sre folla fyda lsysmhla ixfhdackh l< yels h'
    4) Values of a foreign key of a table cannot be updated.
       j.=jl wkH h;=rl (foreign key) w.hhka hdj;ald,Sk l< fkdyels h'
    5) Once created, the structure of a table cannot be altered.
       ks¾udKh l< miqj" j.=jl jHqyh fjkia l< fkdyels h'

                                                                                                ( 2012 – 42 )
    Answer : 3 )

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Option / úl,amh 1)
    Incorrect. In a composite key, none of the columns can have a NULL value because all parts of a primary
    key (whether composite or not) must be NOT NULL.
    jerÈhs' ixhqla; h;=rl" m%d:ñl h;=rl ish¨u fldgia ^ixhqla; fyda fkdjQj;a& NULL fkdúh hq;=
    neúka lsisÿ ;Srejlg NULL w.hla ;sìh fkdyel'

    Option / úl,amh 2)
    Incorrect. Nulls are allowed in non-primary key columns, that are not a part of a primary key.
    jerÈhs' m%d:ñl h;=rl fldgila fkdjk" m%d:ñl fkdjk h;=re ;Srej, Null w.hkag wjir we;'

    Option / úl,amh 3)
    Correct. This is a true and correct statement. A composite primary key is created by combining two or
    more columns.
    ksjerÈhs' fuh i;H iy ksjerÈ m%ldYhls' ;Sre folla fyda jeä .Kkla tAldnoaO lsÍfuka ixhqla;
    m%d:ñl h;=rla ks¾udKh fõ'

    Option / úl,amh 4)
    Incorrect. Values of foreign keys can be updated, provided the new value exists in the referenced primary
    key column.
    jerÈhs' fhduq lrk ,o m%d:ñl h;=re ;Srefõ kj w.h mj;sk ;dla l,a" wd.ka;=l h;=re j, w.hka
    hdj;ald,Sk l< yelsh'

    Option / úl,amh 5)
    Incorrect. In most relational databases, the table structure can be altered using ALTER TABLE command.
    jerÈhs' fndfyda iïnkaO;d o;a; iuqodhkays" ALTER TABLE úOdkh Ndú;fhka j.= jHqyh fjkia
    l< yelsh'

    Therefore, option 3) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 3) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 2. Which of the following is correct with respect to a relational database?
    iïnkaOl o;a; iuqodhla (Relational Database) ms<sn|j i;H jdlH my; i|yka ljrla o?

    1) Any subset of Alternate Keys is called Candidate Key.
       úl,am h;=rej, (Alternative Keys) ´kEu Wml=,lhla wfmalaIl h;=r f,i ye¢kafõ'

    2) A Primary Key is selected from Alternate Keys.
       m%d:ñl h;=r (Primary Key) f;dard .kq ,nkafka úl,am h;=re w;ßka h'

    3) A Foreign Key is an Alternate Key
       wd.ka;=l h;=r (Foreign Key) hkq úl,am h;=rls'

    4) Always Primary Key and Foreign Key are combined to create a Compound Key.
       ieuúgu ixfhdack h;=rla(Compound Key) idod .ekSug m%d:ñl yd wd.ka;=l h;=re ixhqla; flf¾'

    5) Primary Key and Foreign Key establish the relationship between two tables.
       j.= folla w;r iïnkaO;dj m%d:ñl yd wd.ka;=l h;=re u.ska isÿ lrhs'

                                                                                                 ( 2013 – 27 )
    Answer : 5 )
    Explanation / meyeos,s lsrsu
    In relational databases, a Primary Key is a unique identifier for each record in a table, and a Foreign Key
    is a field in one table that refers to the Primary Key in another. These two keys play a critical role in
    maintaining referential integrity and linking data across multiple tables. Without them, there would be no
    systematic way to associate records from one table with those in another.
    iïnkaOl o;a; iuqod (Relational Database) hkays§" m%d:ñl h;=rla hkq j.=jl we;s iEu jd¾;djla
    i|ydu wkkH y÷kd.ekSula jk w;r" wd.ka;=l h;=rla hkq tla j.=jl ;j;a m%d:ñl h;=rla i|yka
    lrk lafIa;%hls' fuu h;=re fol fhduq wLKav;dj mj;ajd .ekSfï§ iy nyq j.= yryd o;a; iïnkaO
    lsÍfï§ ;SrKd;aul ld¾hNdrhla bgq lrhs' tAjd fkdue;sj" tla j.=jl jd¾;d ;j;a j.=jl we;s tAjd
    iuÕ iïnkaO lsÍug l%udkql+, l%uhla fkdue;'

    Option 5 states that the Primary Key and Foreign Key establish the relationship between two tables, which
    is completely accurate. The Foreign Key refers to the Primary Key of another table, creating a bridge
    between both tables. This relationship allows meaningful queries, such as joining tables to find related
    data, which is fundamental in any relational database structure.
    úl,am 5 ys i|yka jkafka m%d:ñl h;=r iy wd.ka;=l h;=r j.= folla w;r iïnkaO;djh ia:dms;
    lrk njhs" th iïmQ¾Kfhkau ksjerÈ h' wd.ka;=l h;=r ;j;a j.=jl m%d:ñl h;=rg fhduq jk
    w;r" j.= folu w;r bridge tlla ks¾udKh lrhs' fuu iïnkaO;djh ´kEu iïnkaO;d o;a; iuqod
    jHqyhl uQ,sl jk wod< o;a; fidhd .ekSug j.= iïnkaO lsÍu jeks w¾:j;a úuiqï j,g bv i,ihs'

    Other options provide incorrect definitions. For example, Candidate Keys are potential Primary Keys, not
    subsets of Alternate Keys. Also, a Foreign Key is not the same as an Alternate Key. Therefore, option 5
    correctly explains the core functionality of relational keys and how they create logical links between
    entities in a database.
    wfkl=;a úl,am ksjerÈ fkdjk w¾:l:k imhhs' WodyrKhla f,i" ksrEmH h;=re hkq úNj m%d:ñl
    h;=re jk w;r úl,am h;=rej, Wm l=,l fkdfõ' tfiau" wd.ka;=l h;=rla úl,am h;=rlg iudk
    fkdfõ' tneúka" úl,am 5 iïnkaOl h;=re j, uQ,sl l%shdldß;ajh iy tAjd o;a; iuqodhl N+;d¾:
    w;r ;d¾lsl ine¢ ks¾udKh lrk wdldrh ksjerÈj meyeÈ,s lrhs'

    Therefore, option 5) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 5) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 3. Consider the following statements regarding relational databases:
    iïnkaOl o;a; iuqod (relational databases) iïnkaOfhka my; § we;s j.ka;s i,lkak(

    A. Changing the column order of relations in a database structure does not necessarily require
       changes in application programs.
       o;a; iuqodhl wdlD;shl iïnkaO;dj, ;Sre mámdáh fjkia lsÍu" fhÿï l%uf,aL ;=< fjkialï
       we;s lsÍug uQ,sl wjYH;djla fkdfõ'
    B. Main objective of normalization of databases is to reduce data redundancy.
       o;a; iuqodj, m%u;lrKfha m%Odk wruqKq jkafka o;a; idßla;;dj (redundancy) wvq lsÍu hs'

    C. Adding new data to the database always requires changes to the existing programs.
       o;a; iuqodhg kj o;a; tl;= lrk iEu wjia:djl § u mj;sk l%uf,aL fjkia lsÍug isÿ fjhs'

    Which of the above statements is/are correct?
    my; i|yka oE w;=ßka ksjerÈ l=ula o?

    1) A only.          2) B only.          3) A and B only.    4) A and C only.      5) All of the above.
       A muKs.             B muKs.             A yd B muKs.        A yd C muKs.          by; ish,a,u.

    Answer: 3)

    Explanation / meyeos,s lsrsu
    Statement A explains that changing the order of columns in a database does not necessarily impact
    application programs, especially if the programs refer to column names rather than positions. This is true
    for modern relational database systems that use SQL with named columns in queries.
    o;a; iuqodhl ;Sre wkqms<sfj, fjkia lsÍu fhÿï jevigyka j,g wksjd¾hfhkau n,mdkafka ke;s
    nj m%ldYh A meyeÈ,s lrhs" úfYaIfhka jevigyka ia:dk fjkqjg ;Sre kï fj; fhduq lrkafka kï'
    úuiqï j, kï lrk ,o ;Sre iys; SQL Ndú;d lrk kùk iïnkaO;d o;a; iuqod moaO;s i|yd fuh
    i;H fõ'

    Statement B highlights the purpose of normalization, which is a process used to minimize data redundancy
    and improve data integrity by organizing tables and relationships logically. This is a fundamental principle
    of relational database design and is widely accepted in practice.
    B m%ldYh idudkHlrKfha wruqK biau;= lrhs" th o;a; w;sßla;h wju lsÍug iy j.= iy
    iïnkaO;d ;d¾lslj ixúOdkh lsÍfuka o;a; wLKav;dj jeä ÈhqKq lsÍug Ndú;d lrk l%shdj,shls'

    ms<s.efka'

    Statement C, however, is incorrect. Adding new data typically involves inserting records and does not
    always require changes to existing programs, unless the data structure or business logic is altered.

    flfia fj;;a" m%ldYh C jerÈhs' kj o;a; tl;= lsÍu idudkHfhka jd¾;d we;=<;a lsÍu we;=<;a jk

   1. Which of the following relations is in the 3rd normal form?
      my; i|yka l=uk iïnkaO;dj (Relation) 3 jk m%u; wdldrfha (3rd normal form) mj;S o?
          1)    student(studentIndexNo, name, parentName)
          2)    sport(sportId, sportName, teacherName, teacherId)
          3)    teacher(teacherId, teacherName, telephoneNumber, subjectName, subjectId)
          4)    book(ISBN, title)
          5)    patient(patientId, patientName, ward, wardId)

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2014 – 30 )
          Answer : 4 )
          Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
          Option/ úl,amh 1)
          studentIndexNo is the primary key. Here, parentName depends on the parent, not directly on the student.
          When there are siblings, the same parentName appears multiple times, creating a hidden transitive
          dependency. This violates 3NF, because parentName should belong to a parent entity, not stored
          redundantly in student. So, this is not in 3NF.
          studentIndexNo hkq m%d:ñl h;=rhs' fuys§" parentName r|d mj;skafka YsIHhd u; fkdj" fouõmshka
          u; h' ifydaor ifydaoßhka isák úg" tlu parentName lsysm j;djla Èiajk w;r" ieÕjqKq ixl%dka;s
          mrhla;;djhla ks¾udKh lrhs' fuh 3NF W,a,x>kh lrhs" ukao parentName, parent N=;d¾:hlg
          wh;a úh hq;= w;r" YsIHhd ;=< w;sßla;j .nvd fkdl< hq;=h' tneúka" fuh 3NF ys fkdue;'

          Option/ úl,amh 2)
          sportId is the key. teacherName depends on teacherId, which is not the key, indicating a transitive
          dependency. So, this is not in 3NF.
          sportId hkq h;=rhs' teacherName hkq teacherId u; r|d mj;S" th h;=r fkdjk w;r th ixl%dka;s
          mrhla;;djla fmkakqï lrhs' tneúka" fuh 3NF ys ke;'

          Option/ úl,amh 3)
          teacherId is the key. subjectName depends on subjectId, which is not the key. So, it is a transitive
          dependency. There is not in 3NF.
          teacherId hkq h;=rhs' subjectName hkq subjectId u; r|d mj;S" th h;=r fkdfõ' tneúka" th
          ixl%dka;s mrhla;;djls' 3NF ys ke;'

          Option/ úl,amh 4)
          ISBN is the key. title is dependent directly on key. So, there are no transitive dependencies. This is in 3NF.
          ISBN hkq h;=rhs' ud;Dldj h;=r u; fl,skau r|d mj;S' tneúka" ixl%dka;s mrhla;;d fkdue;' fuh
          3NF ys we;'

          Option/ úl,amh 5)
          ward depends on wardId, not directly on patientId. There is transitive dependency. So, it is not in 3NF.
          ward" wardId u; r|d mj;S" patientId u; fl,skau fkdfõ' ixl%dka;s mrhla;;djla we;' tneúka" th
          3NF ys ke;'

          Therefore, option 4) is the correct answer.
          tneúka" 4) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 3. Which of the following is the correct primary key for examSubject table?
    my; i|yka oE w;=frka examSubject j.=j i|yd ksjerÈ m%d:ñl h;=r jkafka l=ula o?

    1)   examId
    2)   examId, subjectId
    3)   examId, examDate
    4)   subjectId, examDate
    5)   examId, subjectId, name

    Answer : 2 )

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    Let's analyse the examSubject table to find its primary key.
    examSubject j.=j úYaf,aIKh lr tys m%d:ñl h;=r fidhd .ksuq'

    But there is no single column that uniquely identifies each row. examId alone is not unique, it repeats.
    subjectId and examDate alone are not unique either. But combination of examId and subjectId is unique.
    kuq;a iEu fma<shlau wkkH f,i y÷kd .kakd ;ks ;Srejla fkdue;' examId muKla wkkH fkdfõ"
    th mqkrdj¾;kh fõ' subjectId iy examDate muKla wkkH fkdfõ' kuq;a examId iy subjectId
    ixfhdackh wkkHhs'

    So, the correct primary key is the composite key of examId and subjectId.
    b;ska" ksjerÈ m%d:ñl h;=r examId iy subjectId j, ixhqla; h;=rhs'

    Therefore, option 2) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 2) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

   •      Consider the following relations to answer questions from 36 to 38.
          wxl 01 isg 38 f;la m%Yakj,g ms<s;=re §u i|yd my; olajd we;s iïnkaO;d i,ld n,kak'
          programmer (programmerId, programmerName, gender, NIC, mobilePhoneNumber, degree,
          universityName)
          client(clientId, clientName, address, telephoneNumber)
          project(projectId, projectName, clientId, startDate, endDate, cost)
          workFor(programmerId, projectId, startDate, endDate)
   1. Consider the following statements:
      my; i|yka j.ka;s i,ld n,kak(
          A. A programmer works for at most one project at any given time.
             fok ,o ´kEu wjia:djl§ tla l%uf,aLlhl= jeäukla f,i tla jHdmD;shl (project) jev lrhs'
          B. A programmer is assigned to a single client at any given time.
             tla l%uf,aLlhl= fok ,o ´kEu wjia:djl§ tla fiajd,dNshl= fj; muKla wkqhqla; lrhs'
          C. One client can have more than one project.
             tla fiajd,dNshl= yg tla jHdmD;shlg jvd meje;sh yels h'
          Which of the above statement(s) is/are always correct?
          ieuõg u ksjerÈ jkafka by; i|yka ljr j.ka;sh$j.ka;s o?

          1) A only.                    2) B only.                    3) C only.                    4) A and B only.                  5) B and C only.
             A muKs.                       B muKs.                       C muKs.                       A yd B muKs.                      B yd C muKs.

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2015 – 36 )
          Answer : 3 )
          Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
          Statement / m%ldYh A)
          The existence of a separate table workFor(programmerId, projectId, startDate, endDate)
          shows that each programmer can be assigned to multiple projects, and each project can have multiple
          programmers. This table enables many-to-many mappings. So, a programmer can work on more than one
          project, even at the same time. This, statement A is not always correct.
          fjku workFor(programmerId, projectId, startDate, endDate) j.=jl meje;afuka
          fmkakqï lrkafka tla tla l%uf,aLlhdg jHdmD;s lsysmhla i|yd mejßh yels nj;a" tla tla jHdmD;shg
          l%uf,aLlhska lsysmhla isáh yels nj;ah' fuu j.=j nyq-nyq is;shï.; lsÍï il%Sh lrhs' tneúka"
          l%uf,aLlfhl=g tljr jHdmD;s tllg jvd jev l< yel' fuu m%ldYh A ieuúgu ksjerÈ fkdfõ'
          Statement / m%ldYh B)
          Project belongs to one client (clientId in the project table). However, a programmer can work on
          multiple projects via the workFor table. These multiple projects can belong to different clients. So, a
          programmer could be simultaneously working for multiple clients. Therefore, statement B is also not
          always correct.
          jHdmD;sh tla fiajdodhlfhl=g wh;a fõ ^project j.=fõ clientId&' flfia fj;;a" l%uf,aLlfhl=g
          workFor j.=j yryd jHdmD;s lsysmhl jev l< yelsh' fuu úúO jHdmD;s úúO fiajdodhlhskag
          wh;a úh yelsh' tneúka" l%uf,aLlfhl= tljr fiajdodhlhska lsysmhla i|yd jev l< yelsh' tneúka"
          m%ldYh B o ieuúgu ksjerÈ fkdfõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -19-                                          #ictfromabc


    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    Since the clientId is a foreign key in the project table and not the other way around, this indicates a
    one-to-many (1:N) relationship from client to project. Therefore, a single client can own multiple projects,
    but each project is associated with only one client. So, statement C is always correct.
    clientId hkq project j.=fõ wd.ka;=l h;=rla jk w;r wfkla w;g fkdjk neúka" fuh
    fiajdodhlhdf.ka jHdmD;shg tAl-nyq (1:N) iïnkaO;djhla fmkakqï lrhs' tneúka" ;ks
    fiajdodhlfhl=g jHdmD;s lsysmhla ysñlr .; yels kuq;a" iEu jHdmD;shlau tla fiajdodhlfhl= iuÕ
    muKla iïnkaO fõ' tneúka" m%ldYh C ieuúgu ksjerÈ fõ'

    Therefore, option 3) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 3) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 2. Which of the following is correct with respect to attributes of the relations?
    iïnkaO;dj, Wm,els (attributes) iïnkaOfhka ksjerÈ jkafka my; i|yka ljrla o?

 3. Which of the following is correct?
    my; i|yka j.ka;s w;=frka ljrla ksjerÈ jkafka o?
    1) All relations are in 3rd normal form
       ish¨ iïnkaO;d f;jeks m%u; wjia:dfõ mj;S'
    2) All relations except the programmer are in the 3rd normal form.
       programmer yereKq úg wfkl=;a ish¨ iïnkaO;d f;jeks m%u; wjia:dfõ mj;S'
    3) All relations except the client are in the 3rd normal form.
       client yereKq úg wfkl=;a ish¨ iïnkaO;d f;jeks m%u; wjia:dfõ mj;S'
    4) All relations except the project are in the 3rd normal form.
       project yereKq úg wfkl=;a ish¨ iïnkaO;d f;jeks m%u; wjia:dfõ mj;S'
    5) All relations except the workFor are in the 3rd normal form.
       workFor yereKq úg wfkl=;a ish¨ iïnkaO;d f;jeks m%u; wjia:dfõ mj;S'

                                                                                                ( 2015 – 38 )
    Answer : 2 )
    Let’s analyse the given mappings / § we;s is;shïlrKhka úYaf,aIKh lruq
    programmer(programmerId, programmerName, gender, NIC, mobilePhoneNumber, degree,
    universityName)
    Primary key is programmerId. If attributes like degree depends on universityName, then there is a
    transitive dependency. So, the programmer table is not in 3NF due to potential transitive dependency.
    m%d:ñl h;=r programmerId fõ' Wmdêh jeks .=Kdx. úYaj úoHd, kduh u; r|d mj;S kï" ixl%dka;s
    mrdhla;;djhla we;' tneúka" úNj ixl%dka;s mrdhla;;djhla fya;=fjka programmer j.=j 3NF ys
    fkdue;'

    client(clientId, clientName, address, telephoneNumber)
    Primary key is clientId. All other attributes are directly dependent on the key. So, this is in 3NF.
    m%d:ñl h;=r clientId fõ' wfkl=;a ish¨u .=Kdx. h;=r u; fl,skau r|d mj;S' tneúka" fuh 3NF
    ys we;'

    project(projectId, projectName, clientId, startDate, endDate, cost)
    Primary key is projectId. All attributes fully dependent on projectId. So, this is also in 3NF.
    m%d:ñl h;=r projectId fõ' ish¨u .=Kdx. projectId u; iïmQ¾Kfhkau r|d mj;S' tneúka" fuh 3NF
    ys o we;'

    workFor(programmerId, projectId, startDate, endDate)
    Here, there is a composite key, programmerId and projectId. Non-key attributes, startDate and endDate,
    depend on both of the keys. So, we can assume it is in 3NF.
    fuys§" ixhqla; h;=rla we;" programmerId iy projectId. h;=re fkdjk .=Kdx." startDate iy
    endDate" h;=re folu u; r|d mj;S' tneúka" th 3NF ys we;s nj wmg Wml,amkh l< yelsh'

    From the above analysis, only programmer is not in 3rd normal form.
    by; úYaf,aIKfhka" programmer muKla 3 jk m%u; wdldrfha fkdue;'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -21-                                          #ictfromabc


    Therefore, the correct answer is option 2), All relations except the programmer are in the 3rd normal form.
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r úl,amh 2) fõ" programmer yer wfkl=;a ish¨u iïnkaO;d 3 jk m%u;
    wdldrfha we;'

    Therefore, option 2) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 2) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 4. Consider the following database constraints:
    my; olajd we;s o;a; iuQod ixfrdaOl (constraints) i,ld n,kak(

    A. Primary key / m%d:ñl h;=r
    B. Data type / o;a; m%rEmh
    C. Foreign key / wd.ka;=l h;=r

    Which of the above constraint/s does/do not allow users to duplicate data in a database table?
    o;a; iuqod j.=jl" o;a; wkqmsgm;a (duplicate) lsÍug mßYS,lhkag bv fkdfokq ,nkafka by;
    fmkajd we;s ljr ixfrdaOlh$ixfrdaOl o?

    1) A only.          2) B only.          3) A and B only.   4) A and C only.       5) B and C only.
       A muKs.             B muKs.             A yd B muKs.       A yd C muKs.           B yd C muKs.

                                                                                                  ( 2015 – 39 )
    Answer : 1 )

    Let’s examine the constraints: / iïndOlhka mÍlaId lr n,uq(

    A. Primary key / m%d:ñl h;=r
    Does not allow duplicate values. Each value must be unique.
    wkqmsgm;a w.hkag bv fkdfoa' iEu w.hlau wkkH úh hq;=h'

    B. Data type / o;a; j¾.h
    Does not prevent duplication. It only ensures the kind of data (ex: integer, varchar).
    wkqmsgm;a lsÍu j<lajkafka ke;' th o;a; j¾.h muKla iy;sl lrhs ^Wod( mQ¾K ixLHd"
    varchar&'

    C. Foreign key / wd.ka;=l h;=r
    Allows duplicate values (multiple rows can reference the same value in the parent table).
    wkqmsgm;a w.hkag bv fohs ^nyq fma<s uõ j.=fõ tlu w.h fhduq l< yel&'

    Only the primary key constraint prevents duplication of data in a table. So, the correct answer is A only.
    m%d:ñl h;=re iïndOlh muKla j.=jl o;a; wkqmsgm;a lsÍu j<lajhs' tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r A
    muKs'

    Therefore, option 1) is the correct answer.

 3. Which of the followings is correct with respect to the term ‘domain’ in a relational database?
    iïnkaê; o;a; iuqodhlg (relational database) wkqnoaO j 'jiu' (domain) hk jok i|yd ksjerÈ
    jkafka my; i|yka l=ula o?
    1) It is a set of possible names for a table
       th j.=jla i|yd .; yels kduj, l=,lhls'
    2) It is a set of possible names for an attribute.
       th Wm,elshla (attribute) i|yd .; yels kduj, l=,lhhs'
    3) It is the collection of all possible primary keys

    4) It is the set of all possible values of an attribute
       th Wm,elshlg mej;sh yels ish¨ w.hkaf.a l=,lhhs'
    5) It is the collection of foreign keys

    Explanation of each option / tla tla úl,amh meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    Option / úl,amh 2)
    Incorrect. Attribute names are not domains.
    jerÈhs' Wm,laIK kï jiï fkdfõ'

    Option / úl,amh 3)

    jerÈhs' jiu fï wdldrfhka h;=re iuÕ iïnkaO fkdfõ'

    Option / úl,amh 4)

    attribute can take.

    w.hka iuQyhls'

    jerÈhs' wd.ka;=l h;=re fhduq wLKav;djhg iïnkaO fõ" jiï iuÕ fkdfõ'

    tl national_id_no fõ'

 4. Which of the following Entity Relationship (ER) diagrams correctly represents the relationship
    between the entities represented by the above relations?
    by; iïnkaO;d u.ska ksrEmKh flfrk N+;d¾: w;r iïnkaOh ksjerÈ j ksrEmKh flfrkafka
    my; l=uk N+;d¾: iïnkaO;d (ER) rEm igykska o?

                          1                       m

    2)                    1                       1
             book                   write                 author

    3)                    m                       1
             book                   write                 author

    4)
                          m                       n
             book                   write                 author
    5)
                          m                       m
             book                   write                 author
                                                                                              ( 2017 – 33 )

    Answer : 4 )
    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    have multiple authors and an author can write multiple books.

    ER Notation / ER wxlkh

    •    1 means one / 1 hkq tlla h'
    •    m or n means many / m fyda n hkq fndfyda hkakhs

    ‘m’ to one side and ‘n’ to one side.

    tla me;a;lg ‘m’ iy tla me;a;lg ‘n’ Ndú;d lsÍug isÿfõ'

    tneúka" ksjerÈ ER rEm igyk fmkaúh hq;af;a

    •    book — m side of the relationship / iïnkaO;djfha m me;a;
    •    author — n side of the relationship / iïnkaO;djfha n me;a;

    Therefore, the correct answer is 4).
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r jkafka 4) fõ'

                          2018 DATABASE MCQ MARKING

   1. Consider the following statements regarding databases.
      o;a; iuqod iïnkaOfhka my; oelafjk j.ka;s i,ld n,kak'

          A. Candidate key is a column, or a set of columns that can uniquely identify a row in a table.
             ksrEmH h;=r (candidate key) hkq" j.=jl we;s fma<shla wkkHj y÷kd .ekSug WmldÍ jk
             ;Srejla fyda ;Sre lsysmhls'
          B. Alternate key is any candidate key that has not been selected as the primary key.
             úl,am h;=r (alternate key) hkq m%d:ñl h;=r (primary key) f,i f;dard fkd.;a ksrEmH h;=rls'
          C. Primary key can have a NULL value.
             m%d:ñl h;=r i|yd wNsY=kH (NULL) w.hla ;sìh yels h'

          Which of the above statements is/are correct?
          by; ljr j.ka;shla$j.ka;s ksjerÈ fõ o?

          1) A only.                    2) B only.                    3) A and B only.              4) A and C only.                  5) All A, B and C.
             A muKs.                       B muKs.                       A yd B muKs.                  A yd C muKs.                      A, B iy C ish,a,u'

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2018 – 26 )
          Answer : 3 )

          A candidate key is a set of attributes that can uniquely identify a record in a table. One is chosen as the
          primary key from the set of candidate keys, which is used to identify records uniquely. The remaining
          candidate keys that are not selected as the primary key are called alternate keys.
          wfmalaIl h;=rla hkq j.=jl jd¾;djla wkkH f,i y÷kd.; yels Wm,laIK iuQyhls' jd¾;d wkkH
          f,i y÷kd .ekSug Ndú;d lrk wfmalaIl h;=re lÜg,fhka tlla m%d:ñl h;=r f,i f;dard .kq
          ,efí' m%d:ñl h;=r f,i f;dard fkd.;a b;sß wfmalaIl h;=re úl,am h;=re f,i ye¢kafõ'

          Explanation of each option / tla tla úl,amh meyeÈ,s lsÍu,

          Statement / m%ldYh A)
          This option is correct. A candidate key is a column or a combination of columns that can uniquely identify
          each row in a table. It must contain unique values for every row, meaning two rows cannot have the same
          value. A table can have multiple candidate keys, and each one has the potential to become the primary key.
          fuu úl,amh ksjerÈhs' wfmalaIl h;=rla hkq j.=jl iEu fma<shlau wkkHj y÷kd.; yels ;Srejla
          fyda ;Sre tl;=jls' tys iEu fma<shla i|ydu wkkH w.hka wvx.= úh hq;=h" tkï fma<s follg
          tlu w.hla ;sìh fkdyel' j.=jlg wfmalaIl h;=re lsysmhla ;sìh yels w;r" tA iEu tllau
          m%d:ñl h;=r njg m;aùfï yelshdj we;'

          Statement / m%ldYh B)
          This option is correct. An alternate key refers to any candidate key that has not been chosen as the primary
          key. After identifying all candidate keys, one is selected to become the primary key, and the remaining
          candidate keys are termed alternate keys.
          fuu úl,amh ksjerÈhs' úl,am h;=rla hkq m%d:ñl h;=r f,i f;dardf.k fkdue;s ´kEu wfmalaIl
          h;=rls' ish¨u wfmalaIl h;=re y÷kd .ekSfuka miq" m%d:ñl h;=r njg m;aùug tlla f;dard .kq
          ,nk w;r" b;sß wfmalaIl h;=re úl,am h;=re f,i ye¢kafõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                  -30-                                              #ictfromabc


    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    This option is incorrect. A primary key cannot have NULL values. The purpose of a primary key is to
    uniquely identify each row in a table, and NULL represents an unknown or undefined value, which cannot
    fulfill this purpose.
    fuu úl,amh jerÈhs' m%d:ñl h;=rlg wNsY+kH w.hka ;sìh fkdyel' m%d:ñl h;=rl wruqK j.=jl
    iEu fma<shlau wkkHj y÷kd .ekSu jk w;r" wNsY+kH w.hka uÕska fkdokakd fyda ks¾jpkh fkdl<
    w.hla ksrEmKh flf¾' tu ksid fuu wruqK bgq l< fkdyelsh'

    Therefore, the answer is 3) A and B only
    tneúka ms<s;=r 3) A iy B mu”

 2. Consider the following relational schema in a database.
    o;a; iuqodh moaO;shl my; oelafjk iïnkaO;d mßl%udj (relational schema) i,lkak'

    Subject (SubjectID, TermID, SubjectDescription)

    tys SubjectID, TermID yd SubjectDescription hk oE ............ jk w;r Subject hkak .......... fõ'

    Which of the following are most suitable to fill the blanks, respectively?
    by; ysia;eka msrùu i|yd jvd;a u iqÿiq jkafka ms<sfj<ska my; ljrla o?

    1) attributes, a relation            2) relations, an attribute            3) tuples (records), a relation
       Wm,laIK, iïnkaOhla                   iïnkaO, Wm,laIKhla                    Wm,elshdk, iïnkaOhla

    Answer : 1 )

    Attribute / Wm,laIK
    An attribute refers to a single characteristic of an entity in a database. It is essentially a column in a
    table, and each column defines specific information about the entity represented by the table.
    Wm,laIKhla hkq o;a; iuqodhl we;s N+;d¾:hl ;ks ,laIKhls' th j.=jl ;Srejla jk w;r"
    iEu ;Srejlau j.=fjka ksrEmKh jk N+;d¾:h ms<sn| ksYaÑ; f;dr;=re w¾: olajhs'

    Relation / iïnkaOh
    A relation is a table in the context of a relational database. It is a collection of data that is organized into
    rows and columns.
    iïnkaOhla hkq iïnkaO;d o;a; iuqodhl we;s j.=jls' th fma<s iy ;Sre f,i ixúOdkh lrk ,o
    o;a; tl;=jls'

    Tuple / Wm,elshdk
    A tuple or a record refers to a single row in a relational table. This represents one instance of an entity
    with corresponding values for each attribute.
    Wm,elshdkhla hkq iïnkaO;d j.=jl ;ks fma<shls' fuh tla tla Wm,laIKh i|yd wkqrEm w.hka
    iys; N+;d¾:hl tla wjia:djla ksfhdackh lrhs'

    According to the above explanation/ by; úia;r lsßug wkqj,

    Subject refers to the relation or a table, which organizes data about different subjects. The SubjectID,
    TermID, and SubjectDescription are attributes or columns of this relation, representing specific
    characteristics of each subject.
    Subject hkafkka woyia lrkafka úúO úIhhka ms<sn| o;a; ixúOdkh lrk iïnkaOhla fyda j.=jla
    h' SubjectID, TermID iy SubjectDescription hkq fuu iïnkaOfha Wm,laIK fyda ;Sre jk w;r"
    tla tla úIhfha ksYaÑ; ,laIK ksfhdackh lrhs'

 3. Consider the following statements regarding databases.
    o;a; iuqodhla ms<sn| my; m%ldY i,lkak'
    A. For each attribute of a relation. there is a set of permitted values, called the domain of that attribute

    Which of the above statements is/are correct?

    Answer : 3 )

    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    This option is correct. In a relational database, each attribute in a table has a domain, which refers to a set
    of valid values that an attribute in a table can take. It defines the data types and range of values for a
    specific attribute.
    fuu úl,amh ksjerÈhs' iïnkaO;d o;a; iuqodhl" j.=jl we;s iEu Wm,laIKhlgu jiula we;"
    th j.=jl we;s Wm,laIKhlg .; yels j,x.= w.hka iuQyhlah' th ksYaÑ; Wm,laIKhla i|yd
    o;a; j¾. iy w.hka mrdih ks¾jpkh lrhs'
    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    This option is incorrect. In relational databases, tuples or records in a relation or a table are not inherently
    sorted. The database does not impose any ordering of the rows unless requested by the user. Then the
    records can be sorted using SQL queries such as ORDER BY. By default, the rows in a table are not in
    any specific order.
    fuu úl,amh jerÈhs' iïnkaOl o;a; iuqodhka ;=<" iïnkaOhl fyda j.=jl we;s Wm,elshdk iEu
    úgu wkqms<sfj,g ilid fkdue;' mßYS,lhd b,a,d isák ;=re o;a; iuqodhl fma<s wkqms<sfj,la
    mkjkafka ke;' túg ORDER BY jeks SQL úuiqï Ndú;fhka jd¾;d wkqms<sfj,lg ielish yel'
    fmrksñfhka" j.=jl fma<s lsisÿ ksYaÑ; wkqms<sfj,lg fkdue;'
    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    This option is correct. A database schema is essentially the blueprint of the database, describing the
    structure of the database, including the tables, relationships, attributes, and constraints. It defines how the
    data is organized and how the relationships between data are structured.
    fuu úl,amh ksjerÈhs' o;a; iuqod mßl%udjla hkq o;a; iuqodfha ie,eiau jk w;r th j.="

 4. Consider the following statements.
    my; m%ldY i,lkak'
     A. Students relation is not normalized
        Students iïnkaOh m%u;lrKh (normalized) lr ke;'
     B. Students is a relation in First Normal Form (1NF) only.
        Students hkq m<uq m%u;lrKfhys (First Normal Form - 1NF) muKla we;s iïnkaOhls'
     C. In normalization terms, Students is a relation in Second Normal Form (2NF) and hence also in
        1NF. / m%u;lrK w¾:j,g wkqj Students hkq fojk m%u;lrKfhys (Second Normal Form -
        2NF) ys we;s tkhska 1NF ys o we;s iïnkaOhls'
     Which of the above statements is/are correct?
     by; m%ldYj,ska ljrla ksjerÈ fõ o?

     1) A only.          2) B only.           3) A and C only.     4) B and C only.       5) All A, B and C.
        A muKs.             B muKs.              A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.           A, B iy C ish,a,u'

                                                                                                       ( 2018 – 41 )
     Answer: ALL)

     A table is in First Normal Form (1NF) if all the columns contain atomic values (no repeating groups or
     multi-valued attributes).
     j.=jl ish¨u ;Srej, mrudKql w.hka ^mqkrdj¾;k lKavdhï fyda nyq w.hka we;s Wm,laIK
     fkdue;& wvx.= kï" th m<uq m%u; wjia:dfjka (1NF) we;'

     A table is in Second Normal Form (2NF) if it has no partial dependencies, which means all non-prime
     attributes are fully functionally dependent on the entire primary key.
     j.=jlg wdxYsl mrdh;a;;d fkdue;s kï th fojk m%u; wjia:dfjka (2NF) we;" tkï ish¨u m%d:ñl
     fkdjk Wm,laIK, m%d:ñl h;=r u; iïmQ¾Kfhkau l%shdldÍj r|d mj;S'

     A table is in Third Normal Form (3NF) if it has no transitive dependencies. In other words, a non-prime
     attribute must depend only on the primary key, and not on another non-prime attribute.
     ixl%dka;s mrdh;a;;d fkdue;s kï j.=jla ;=kajk m%u; wjia:dfjka (3NF) we;' fjk;a jpk j,ska
     lsjfyd;a" m%d:ñl fkdjk Wm,laIKhla m%d:ñl h;=r u; muKla r|d mj;sk w;r ;j;a m%d:ñl
     fkdjk Wm,laIKhla u; r|d fkdmj;S'

     Statement / m%ldYh A)
     This is incorrect. Student table satisfies the First Normal Form as all attributes contain atomic values, and
     it also satisfies Second Normal Form, since there are no partial dependencies.
     fuh jerÈhs' ish¨u Wm,laIKj, mrudKql w.hka wvx.= jk neúka Students j.=j m<uq m%u;
     wjia:dj ;Dma;su;a lrk w;r wdxYsl mrdh;a;;d fkdue;s neúka th fojk m%u; wjia:djo ;Dma;su;a
     lrhs'

     But there is a transitive dependency because postal_code depends on postal_town, Therefore the table is
     in 2NF. The table is normalized, and the statement that it is "not normalized" is incorrect.
     kuq;a postal_code Wm,laIKh" postal_town u; r|d mj;sk ksid ixl%dka;s mrdh;a;;djla we;" tneúka
     j.=j 2NFys we;' j.=j m%u;lrKh lr we;s w;r" th "m%u;lrKh lr ke;" hk m%ldYh jerÈh'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -33-                                          #ictfromabc


    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    This option is incorrect. The Students table is in first normal form since there are no repeating groups or
    multi-valued attributes. But it is also in second normal form.
    fuu úl,amh jerÈhs' mqkrdj¾;k lKavdhï fyda nyq w.hka we;s Wm,laIK fkdue;s neúka Students
    j.=j m<uq m%u; wjia:dfjka mj;S' kuq;a th fojk m%u; wjia:dfjkao we;'

    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    This option is correct. Even though postal_code depends on postal_town, this is a transitive dependency,
    not a partial dependency, so it doesn't violate 2NF. Therefore Student table is in Second Normal Form
    which means the table is also in First Normal Form.
    fuu úl,amh ksjerÈhs' postal_code Wm,laIKh postal_town u; r|d mej;=ko" fuh ixl%dka;s
    mrdh;a;;djla jk w;r wdxYsl mrdh;a;;djla fkdfõ" tneúka th 2NF W,a,x>kh fkdlrhs' Students
    j.=j fojk m%u; wjia:dfjka we;" tkï j.=j m<uq m%u; wjia:dfjkao we;'

    The correct answer is not in the given answers. The correct answer is C only.
    § we;s ms<s;=rej, ksjerÈ ms<s;=r ioyka fkdfõ' ksjerÈ ms<s;=r C mu”'

 5. Consider the following statements relating to Entity Relationship (ER) modelling.
    N=;d¾: iïnkaO;d (ER) wdlD;slrKh ms<sn| my; oelafjk m%ldY i,lkak'

    A. Cardinality specifies how many instances of an entity relates to one instance of another entity.
       .KkSh;dj (cardinality) u.ska tla N+;d¾:hl (entity) oDIagdka; (instances) fldmuK ixLHdjla
       fjk;a N+;d¾:hl tla oDIagdka;hlg iïnkaO fõ oehs úfYaIfhka i|yka lrkq ,efí'
    B. An entity is a ‘thing’ or ‘object’ in the real world that can be identified separately (distinguishable)
       from all other objects.
       N+;d¾:hla hkq ;:H f,dalfhys (real world) we;s wfkla ish¨ jia;+kaf.ka fjkalr y÷kd .;
       yelsehï ‘fohla’ fyda ‘jia;=jla’ fyda fõ'
    C. Cardinality specifies primary key attributes of an entity.
       .KkSh;dj u.ska N+;d¾:hl m%d:ñl h;=f¾ Wm,laIK úfYaIs;j oelafõ'

    Which of the above is/are correct?
    by; ljrla ksjerÈ fõ o?

    1) A only.           2) A and B only.     3) A and C only.     4) B and C only.     5) All of the above.
       A muKs.              A yd B muKs.         A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.         by; ish,a,u.

                                                                                                   ( 2018 – 42 )
    Answer : 2 )
    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    This statement is correct. Cardinality refers to the number of instances of one entity that are related to an
    instance of another entity. In Entity-Relationship (ER) diagrams, cardinality defines the relationships
    between entities, such as one-to-one, one-to-many, or many-to-many. It tells how many instances of one
    entity can be associated with an instance of another.
    fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈhs' .KkSh;djh hkq tla N+;d¾:hla ;j;a N+;d¾:hl oDIagdka;hlg iïnkaO jk
    wjia:d .Kkhs' N+;d¾: iïnkaO;d (ER) rEm igyka j,§" .KkSh;djh tAl tAl" tAl nyq fyda nyq
    nyq f,i N+;d¾: w;r iïnkaO;d w¾: olajhs' th tla N+;d¾:hl oDIagdka; lSmhla ;j;a N+;d¾:hl
    oDIagdka;hla iuÕ iïnkaO l< yelsoehs fmkajd fohs'
    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    This statement is correct. In database design, an entity is an object or concept that is distinguishable from
    others and has meaningful attributes. Each entity has attributes that describe it, and it can be uniquely
    identified, often through a primary key in a database. This helps in separating one instance of an entity
    from another.
    fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈhs' o;a; iuqod ks¾udKfha§" N+;d¾:hla hkq wfkla tAjdhska fjkalr y÷kd.;
    yels iy w¾:j;a Wm,laIK we;s jia;=jla fyda ixl,amhls' iEu N+;d¾:hlgu th úia;r lrk
    Wm,laIK we;s w;r" th fndfyda úg o;a; iuqodhl m%d:ñl h;=rla yryd wkkH f,i y÷kd.;
    yelsh' fuh N+;d¾:hl tla oDIagdka;hla ;j;a N+;d¾:hlska fjkalr y÷kd .ekSug WmldÍ fõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -34-                                           #ictfromabc


    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    This statement is incorrect. Cardinality does not specify the primary key attributes of an entity. Cardinality
    refers to the relationship between entities, as explained in option A. Primary keys are attributes that
    uniquely identify instances of an entity within a table.
    fuu m%ldYh jerÈhs' .KkSh;djh u.ska N+;d¾:hl m%d:ñl h;=re Wm,laIK ksYaÑ;j olajd ke;'
    úl,am A ys meyeÈ,s lr we;s mßÈ" .KkSh;djh hkq N+;d¾: w;r iïnkaO;djhhs' m%d:ñl h;=re
    hkq j.=jla ;=< N+;d¾:hl oDIagdka; wkkH f,i y÷kd .kakd Wm,laIK fõ'

    Therefore the answer is 2) A and B only
    tneúka ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 2) A iy B mu”

                          2019 DATABASE MCQ MARKING

   •      Consider the following database table to answer the questions 01 to 02.
          wxl 01 isg 02 m%Yak i|yd ms<s;=re iemhSug my; oelafjk o;a; iuqod j.=j i,lkak'

                             Student_Sport
              Student_Id Event_Id Event_Name
                10012      S-02   Carrom
                10022      S-01   Basketball
                10018      S-02   Carrom
                10012      S-03   Volleyball
                10025      S-04   Chess
                10018      S-01   Basketball

   1. In which normal form does the above table exist?
      by; j.=j mj;skafka l=uk m%u; wdldrhg o?

          1)    BCNF /
          2)    First normal form / m%:u m%u; wdldrh
          3)    Second normal form / fojk m%u; wdldrh
          4)    Third normal form / ;=kajk m%u; wdldrh
          5)    Zero normal form / Y=kH m%u; wdldrh

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2019 – 15 )
          Answer : 2 )
          Step 1: Identify Functional Dependencies / l%shdldÍ hemSï y÷kd .kak

          The table consists of:
          j.=j iukaú; jkafka(
          •     Student_Id – Unique identifier for students / isiqka i|yd wkkH y÷kd.ekSula
          •     Event_Id – Unique identifier for events / isÿùï i|yd wkkH y÷kd.ekSu
          •     Event_Name – Name of the event / isoaêfha ku

          From the data, we can identify the following functional dependencies (FDs).
          o;a; j,ska" wmg my; l%shdldÍ mrdh;a;;d y÷kd.; yel'
          1. Event_Id → Event_Name (Each event has a unique name iEu isÿùulgu wkkH kula we;)
          2. (Student_Id, Event_Id) → Event_Name (A combination of Student_Id and Event_Id determines
             Event_Name. Student_Id iy Event_Id j, tl;=jla Event_Name ;SrKh lrhs)

          Step 2: Check for Normal Forms / m%u: wdldr i|yd mÍlaId lrkak
          1NF (First Normal Form) – m%:u m%u; wdldrh (Option 2)
          A table is in 1NF if:

          •     No repeating groups or arrays: The table should only have atomic column values.
                mqkrdj¾;k lKavdhï fyda arrays fkdue;( j.=fõ wxY=uh ;Sre w.hka muKla ;sìh hq;=h'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -36-                                        #ictfromabc


        •   There are no repeating groups or sets of related columns.
            mqkrdj¾;k lKavdhï fyda wod< ;Sre fkdue;'

    The given table is already in 1NF because every column holds single atomic values, and no repeating
    groups exist.
    iEu ;Srejlau ;ks mrudKql w.hka orK ksid;a" mqkrdj¾;k lKavdhï fkdue;s ksid;a § we;s j.=j
    oekgu;a 1NF ys we;'

    3) 2NF (Second Normal Form) – fojk m%u; wdldrh (Option 3)

    A table is in 2NF if,
    j.=jla 2NF wdldrfhka ;sfí kï"

    •   It is in 1NF.
        th 1NF ys ;sìh hq;=h'
    •   There are no partial dependencies (non-key attributes must be fully dependent on entire primary key).
        wdxYsl mrdhla;;d fkdue; (h;=re fkdjk Wm,laIK iïmQ¾K m%d:ñl h;=r u; iïmQ¾Kfhkau
        r|d mej;sh hq;=h')
    If the primary key is (Student_Id, Event_Id),
    (Student_Id, Event_Id) m%d:ñl h;=r kï"

    •   Event_Name depends only on Event_Id, not the full primary key (Student_Id, Event_Id).
        Event_Name r|d mj;skafka Event_Id u; ñi iïmQ¾K m%d:ñl h;=r u; fkdfõ (Student_Id,
        Event_Id)
    •   This is a partial dependency, violating 2NF.
        fuh 2NF W,a,x>kh lrñka isÿjk wdxYsl mrdhla;;djhls'

    The table does not satisfy 2NF because of the partial dependency.
    wdxYsl mrdhla;;dj ksid j.=j 2NF ;Dma;su;a fkdfõ'

    4) 3NF (Third Normal Form) – ;=kajk m%u; wdldrh (Option 4)

    A table is in 3NF if,
    j.=jla 3NF wdldrfhka ;sfí kï"

    •   It is in 2NF.
        th 2NF ys ;sìh hq;=h'
    •   There are no transitive dependencies (non-key attributes must depend only on the primary key, not on
        other non-key attributes).
        ixl%dka;s mrdh;a;;d fkdue; ^m%Odk fkdjk .=Kdx. m%d:ñl h;=r u; muKla r|d mej;sh hq;=h"
        fjk;a m%Odk fkdjk .=Kdx. u; fkdfõ&'

    Since the table does not satisfy 2NF, it automatically fails 3NF as well.
    j.=j 2NF ;Dma;su;a fkdjk neúka" th iajhxl%Shj 3NF o wiu;a fõ'

    The table is not in 3NF.
    j.=j 3NF ys fkdue;'

    5) Obviously this does not hold in 0NF. (Option 5)
       fuh 0NF ys fkdmj;sk nj meyeÈ,sh'

 3. Consider the following statements regarding the Extended Entity Relationship (EER) model.
    úia;D; N+;d¾: iïnkaO;d (EER) wdlD;sh ms<sn| my; oelafjk j.ka;s i,lkak'

    A. EER model includes all the concepts of the original ER model.
       EER wdlD;shg uq,a ER wdlD;sfhys we;s ish¨ u ixl,am we;=<;a fõ'
    B. EER model has additional concepts of specialization/generalization.
       úfYaIlrKh$idudkHlrKh ms<sn| wu;r ixl,am EER ys mj;S'
    C. EER model includes a new concept to model the weak entities.
       ÿ¾j, N+;d¾: wdlD;slrKh i|yd kj ixl,amhla EER ys we;=<;a fõ'

    Which of the above statements is/are correct?
    by; l=uk j.ka;shla$j.ka;s ksjerÈ fõ o?

    1) A only.         2) B only.          3) A and B only. 4) A and C only.       5) All A, B and C.
       A muKs.            B muKs.             A iy B muKs'     A iy C muKs'           A, B iy C ish,a,u'

                                                                                               ( 2019 – 16 )
    Answer : 3 )
    What is the Extended Entity Relationship (EER) model?
    úia;D; N+:d¾; iïnkaO;d (EER) wdlD;sh hkq l=ulao @

    The Extended Entity Relationship (EER) model is an enhanced version of the original Entity Relationship
    (ER) model. It includes all the features of the ER model but introduces additional concepts for modeling
    complex data relationships. These enhancements make the EER model more powerful and flexible,
    accommodating various advanced requirements in database design. Some of these enhancements include
    concepts like specialization, generalization, and modeling weak entities, which allow a more nuanced
    representation of real-world scenarios.
    úia;D; N+:d¾; iïnkaO;d (EER) wdlD;sh hkq uq,a N+:d¾; iïnkaO;d ^ER& wdlD;sfha jeä ÈhqKq l<
    wkqjdohls' thg ER wdlD;sfha ish¨u úfYaIdx. we;=<;a jk kuq;a ixlS¾K o;a; iïnkaO;d wdlD;s
    ks¾udKh i|yd wu;r ixl,am y÷kajd fohs' fuu jeäÈhqKq lsÍï EER wdlD;sh jvd;a n,j;a yd
    kuHYS,S lrhs" o;a; iuqod ks¾udKfha úúO Wiia wjYH;d imqrd,hs' fuu jeäÈhqKq lsÍïj,ska iuyrla
    úfYaISlrKh" idudkHlrKh iy ÿ¾j, N+:d¾; wdlD;s ks¾udKh jeks ixl,am we;=<;a jk w;r
    tuÕska ienE f,dal wjia:d jvd;a ishqï f,i ksrEmKh lsÍug bv i,ihs'
    Statement A
    Correct, The EER model is an extension of the ER model, meaning it includes all its fundamental concepts
    like entities, attributes, relationships, and weak entities. / ksjerÈ" EER wdlD;sh ER wdlD;sfha È.=jls"
    tkï thg N+;d¾;" Wm,laIK" iïnkaO;d iy ÿ¾j, N+;d¾; jeks tys ish¨ uQ,sl ixl,am we;=<;afõ'
    Statement B
    Correct, The EER model introduces new concepts such as.
    ksjerÈ" EER wdlD;sh kj ixl,am y÷kajd fohs'
    Specialization (a higher-level entity is divided into lower-level sub-entities).
    úfYaISlrKh ^by< uÜgfï N+;d¾;hla my< uÜgfï Wm N+;d¾;j,g fnod we;&'
    Generalization (lower-level entities are merged into a higher-level entity).
    idudkHlrKh ^my< uÜgfï N+;d¾; by< uÜgfï N+;d¾;hlg tAldnoaO lr we;&'
    Statement C
    Incorrect, Weak entities were already part of the original ER model.
    jerÈ" ÿ¾j, N+;d¾; oekgu;a uq,a ER wdlD;sfha fldgila úh'
    The EER model does not introduce weak entities as a new concept, but it enhances relationships with
    additional constraints like categorization and aggregation.
    EER wdlD;sh ÿ¾j, N+;d¾; kj ixl,amhla f,i y÷kajd fkdfoa" kuq;a th j¾.SlrKh iy tl;=
    lsÍu jeks wu;r iSudjka iuÕ in|;d jeä ÈhqKq lrhs'

   1. Which of the following statement/s about a relation in the Second Normal Form are true?
      fojk m%u;lrKfhys (2NF) we;s iïnkaO;djhla ms<sn|j my; ljr j.ka;s$h i;H fõ o?
          A. It can have a composite key.
             thg ixhqla; h;=rla ;sìh yel'
          B. It should be in the First Normal Form (1NF) as well.
             th m<uq m%u;lrKfhys (1NF) o mej;sh hq;= h'
          C. All non-key attributes are fully functionally dependent on the primary key.
             h;=re fkdjk ish¨ Wm,els m%d:ñl h;=r u; uq¿ukskau ld¾hnoaOjemrdh;a; fõ'

          1) B only.                     2) C only.                      3) A and B only.               4) B and C only.                5) All of the above.
             B muKs.                        C muKs.                         A yd B muKs.                   B yd C muKs.                    by; ish,a,u.

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2020 – 27 )
          Answer : 4 )
          Let’s go through the given statements to find the correct answer.
          ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fidhd .ekSug § we;s m%ldYhka yryd huq'

          A. It can have a composite key
             thg ixhqla; h;=rla ;sìh yel'

                True: A relation in Second Normal Form (2NF) can have a composite key. In fact, 2NF often
                deals with cases where there is a composite key, ensuring that all non-key attributes are fully
                dependent on the entire composite key, not just part of it.
                i;H fõ: m%u;lrKfha (2NF) iïnkaO;djhlg ixhqla; h;=rla ;sìh yel' we;a; jYfhkau" 2NF
                fndfyda úg ixhqla; h;=rla we;s wjia:d iuÕ lghq;= lrhs" ish¨u h;=re- fkdjk Wm,laIK tys
                fldgila muKla fkdj iïmQ¾K ixhqla; h;=r u; mQ¾K mrdhla;jfhka hqla; nj iy;sl lrhs'

          B. It should be in the First Normal Form (1NF) as well
             th m<uq m%u;lrKfhys (1NF) o mej;sh hq;= h'

                True: For a relation to be in 2NF, it must first satisfy the conditions of 1NF. This means the table
                must have no repeating groups and each column must contain atomic (indivisible) values.
                i;H fõ: 2NF ys iïnkaO;djhla i|yd" th m<uqj 1NF ys fldkafoais imqrd,sh hq;=h' fuhska
                woyia lrkafka j.=fõ mqkrdj¾;k lKavdhï fkd;sìh hq;= w;r iEu ;Srejlu mrudKql ^fnÈh
                fkdyels& w.hka wvx.= úh hq;= njhs'

          C. All non-key attributes are fully functionally dependent on the primary key
             h;=re fkdjk ish¨ Wm,els (attributes) m%d:ñl h;=r u; uq¿ukskau ld¾hnoaOj mrdh;a; fõ'
                True: 2NF requires that all non-key attributes be fully functionally dependent on the entire primary
                key, not just part of it (this is especially relevant in cases where the key is composite).
                i;H fõ: 2NF g wjYH jkafka ish¨u m%Odk fkdjk Wm,laIK tys fldgila muKla fkdj
                iïmQ¾K m%d:ñl h;=r u; iïmQ¾Kfhkau ld¾hnoaOj mrdh;a; ùuhs' ^h;=r ixhqla; jk wjia:d
                j,§ fuh úfYaIfhka wod< fõ&'

          Therefore, the correct answer is, 5).
          tneúka, ksjerÈ ms<s;=r" 5) fõ'

 2. Which of the following statement/s regarding the logical database schema are true?
    ;d¾lsl o;a; iuqodh l%udkqrEm(logical database shema) ms<sn|j my; ljr j.ka;s i;H fõo?
    A. It is a blueprint for a database.
       th o;a; iuqodhla i|yd jk ie,iqul msgm;ls (blueprint).
    B. It contains data and information.
       tys o;a; iy f;dr;=re wka;¾.; fõ'
    C. It formulates all the constraints that are to be applied on the data.
       th o;a; flfrys fhfokakd jQ ish¨ ixfrdaOl (constraints) úêu;aj olajhs'

    1) A only.           2) A and B only.    3) A and C only.     4) B and C only.     5) All of the above.
       A muKs.              A yd B muKs.        A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.         by; ish,a,u.

                                                                                                  ( 2020 – 28 )
    Answer : 3 )

    In database management, the logical schema plays a critical role in designing and organizing the structure
    of a database. It serves as a blueprint that defines how data is logically stored, the relationships between
    different data entities, and the constraints that ensure data integrity.
    o;a; iuqod l<ukdlrKfha§" o;a; iuqodfha jHqyh ie,iqï lsÍfï§ iy ixúOdkh lsÍfï§ ;d¾lsl
    ie,eiau ;SrKd;aul ld¾hNdrhla bgq lrhs' th o;a; ;d¾lslj .nvd lrk wdldrh" úúO o;a;
    N=;d¾: w;r iïnkaO;d iy o;a; wLKav;dj iy;sl lrk iSudjka ks¾jpkh lrk ie,eiaula f,i
    fiajh lrhs'

    A. It is a blueprint for a database
       th o;a; iuqodhla i|yd jk ie,iqul msgm;ls (blueprint)
        True: A logical database schema is indeed a blueprint or design for the structure of a database. It
        outlines how the data is organized, including the tables, relationships, fields, and data types.
        i;H fõ: ;d¾lsl o;a; iuqod fhdackd l%uhla hkq ienúkau o;a; iuqodhl jHqyh i|yd ie,eiaula
        fyda ie,iquls' j.=" iïnkaO;d" lafIa;% iy o;a; j¾. we;=¿j o;a; ixúOdkh ù we;s wdldrh
        th f.kyer olajhs'

    B. It contains data and information
       tys o;a; iy f;dr;=re wka;¾.; fõ'
        False: A logical schema does not contain data or information. Instead, it defines the structure and
        rules of the database, while the actual data is stored in the physical database.
        wi;Hh fõ: ;d¾lsl ie,eiaul o;a; fyda f;dr;=re wvx.= fkdfõ' tA fjkqjg" th o;a; iuqodfha
        jHqyh iy Í;s ks¾jpkh lrhs" i;H o;a; fN!;sl o;a; .nvdfõ .nvd lr we;'

    C. It formulates all the constraints that are to be applied on the data.
       th o;a; flfrys fhfokakd jQ ish¨ ixfrdaOl (constraints) úêu;aj olajhs'
        True: A logical schema defines the constraints that ensure data integrity, such as primary keys,
        foreign keys, uniqueness, and other rules that are enforced on the data.
        i;H fõ: m%d:ñl h;=re" úfoaYSh h;=re" wkkH;dj iy o;a; u; n,d;aul lrk wfkl=;a kS;s
        jeks o;a; wLKav;dj iy;sl lrk iSudjka ;d¾lsl l%uf,aLhla w¾: olajhs'

    The statements A and C are correct.
    A iy C m%ldY ksjerÈ fõ.

    Therefore, the correct answer is, 3).
    tneúka, ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 3) fõ'

   1. Which of the following is a (are) good practice(s) to follow in database development?
      o;a; iuqodhla iE§fu§ ms<smeÈh hq;= fyd| mqreoaola$mqreÿ jkafka my; ljrla o?

 2. Which of the following is most suited to be selected as the primary key of the Results table with
    respect to the given details?
    § we;s úia;r wkqj" Results j.=fõ m%d:ñl h;=r f,i f;dard .ekSug my; ljrla jvd;au iqÿiq fõ o?
     1) NIC                                   2) SubjectID                              3) StudentNo
     4) StudentNo and NIC                     5) StudentNo and SubjectID

     Answer : 5 )
     Primary Key / m%d:ñl h;=r
     A field or combination of fields used to uniquely identify records in a table is called a primary key.
     j.=jl jd¾;d wkkHj y÷kd .ekSug Ndú;d lrk lafIa;%hla fyda lafIa;% lsysmhl tl;=jla m%d:ñl
     h;=rla f,i y÷kajhs'
     Data in a primary key must be unique and must not contain empty values.
     m%d:ñl h;=rl o;a; wksjdrfhkau wkkH úh hq;= w;r ysia w.hka mej;sh hq;= ke;'
     A primary key is selected from among the candidate keys and the candidate keys that are not selected as
     the primary key are called alternate keys.
     m%d:ñl h;=rla f;dard .kq ,nkafka wfmalaIl h;=re w;ßka jk w;r m%d:ñl h;=r f,i f;dard
     fkd.kakd ,o wfmalaIl h;=re úl,am h;=re f,i y÷kajkq ,nhs'
     Looking at the Results table in the question, you can see that a single field cannot be used as the primary
     key. This is because the data is repeated in each field.
     m%Yakfha i|yka Results j.=j ms,sn|j wjOdkh fhduq lsÍfï§ ;ks lafIa;%hla m%d:ñl h;=r f,i
     Ndú; l< fkdyels nj y÷kd .; yel' Bg fya;=j jkafka iEu lafIa;%hlu o;a; kej; kej; fh§
     ;sîuhs'
     Therefore, 2 or more existing fields in the table must be used to create a primary key.
     tuksid" j.=fõ mj;sk lafIa;% 2 la fyda lsysmhla ydú; lr m%d:ñl h;=rla ks¾udKh lr .; hq;= fõ'
     Below are the keys that can be created in this way.
     tfia ks¾udKh lr .; yels h;=re my;ska fõ'
     StudentNo + SubjectID
     NIC + SubjectID
     Accordingly, the answer of the question is StudentNo and SubjectID, so the answer is 5).
     tA wkqj m%Yakfha ms<s;=re j, i|yka jkafka StudentNo iy SubjectID neúka ms<s;=r 5) fõ'

     Important concepts / jeo.;a ixl,am
     •   Fully functional dependency / mq¾K ld¾hnoaO mrdhla;;d
         A non-key attribute should be dependent on the whole primary key, not just part of it.
         h;=r fkdjk Wm,laIKhla tys fldgila muKla fkdj iïmQ¾K m%d:ñl h;=r u; r|d mej;sh hq;=h'
     •   Candidate key / wfmalaIl h;=r
